Additionally, the `presets='extreme'` setting combined with a `time_limit=300` is highly aggressive for a 10-fold CV loop, likely leading to timeouts or resource contention in the benchmark environment.

While this is intended to fit within the budget, AutoGluon's `extreme` preset is extremely resource-intensive and often triggers internal memory management issues or process timeouts when running 10 folds in a restricted environment. Furthermore, the `path` attribute in `AutoGluonModel` creates a new directory for every fold, which can lead to disk space exhaustion or file system overhead in a shared environment. Most importantly, the `build_model` function returns an instance of `AutoGluonModel`, but the `fit` method returns `self` (which is correct), but the `predict` method relies on `self.predictor` which is only populated after `fit`. If the evaluation harness calls `predict` before `fit` or in a way that doesn't persist the state correctly across the cross-validation loop, it will fail.
